登革热后的侵袭性曲霉病:尸检病例和文献综述。

Invasive Aspergillosis Following Dengue: An Autopsy Case and Review of the Literature.
作者： Avichal Rajpal, Immanuvel Jebaraj, Inderpaul Singh Sehgal, Kritarth Rathi, Sangamitra Rajasekaran, Uma Nahar, Kuruswamy Thurai Prasad, Shivaprakash M Rudramurthy, Ritesh Agarwal, Valliappan Muthu
摘要中文翻译侵袭性曲霉病通常发生在免疫功能低下的 宿主 中,但在病毒性疾病(包括登革热和 2019 年冠状病毒病)之后,人们越来越多地认识到侵袭性曲霉病。本文描述了 重症登革热 后传播性曲霉病的致命病例,并对这种关联进行了系统评价。一名先前健康的 25 岁女性出现发烧和多器官功能障碍。登革热 IgM 结果呈阳性,排除其他热带感染。尽管接受了重症监护,患者还是出现了进行性呼吸衰竭、难治性休克和急性肾损伤,最终死亡。尸检显示弥漫性肺泡损伤和广泛的侵袭性曲霉菌病,累及支气管、肺血管系统和心肌。在本次系统评价中,发现了另外 21 例登革热后侵袭性曲霉病病例(总数 = 22;中位年龄 36 岁,59% 男性),主要发生在亚洲。15 例(68%)有肺部受累。大多数患者患有 重症登革热 并伴有多器官功能障碍。总体死亡率为63.6%;23%的病例是尸检确诊的。侵袭性曲霉病并发 重症登革热 拓宽了病毒相关曲霉病的范围。

Invasive Aspergillosis Following Dengue: An Autopsy Case and Review of the Literature.

Authors: Avichal Rajpal, Immanuvel Jebaraj, Inderpaul Singh Sehgal, Kritarth Rathi, Sangamitra Rajasekaran, Uma Nahar, Kuruswamy Thurai Prasad, Shivaprakash M Rudramurthy, Ritesh Agarwal, Valliappan Muthu
Original abstract (English)
Invasive aspergillosis typically occurs in immunocompromised hosts but has been increasingly recognized following viral illnesses, including dengue and coronavirus disease 2019. Herein, a fatal case of disseminated aspergillosis following severe dengue is described, and the association is systematically reviewed. A previously healthy 25-year-old woman presented with fever and multiorgan dysfunction. Dengue IgM results were positive, and other tropical infections were excluded. Despite intensive care, the patient developed progressive respiratory failure, refractory shock, and acute kidney injury and died. An autopsy revealed diffuse alveolar damage and extensive invasive aspergillosis involving the bronchi, pulmonary vasculature, and myocardium. In the present systematic review, 21 additional cases of invasive aspergillosis following dengue (total n = 22; median age 36 years, 59% male) were identified, predominantly in Asia. Fifteen cases (68%) had pulmonary involvement. Most patients had severe dengue with multiorgan dysfunction. The overall mortality was 63.6%; 23% of cases were diagnosed postmortem. Invasive aspergillosis complicating severe dengue broadens the spectrum of viral-associated aspergillosis.
View review five-element analysis

Invasive aspergillosis typically occurs in immunocompromised hosts but has been increasingly recognized following viral illnesses, including dengue and coronavirus disease 2019. Evidence base and method
In the present systematic review, 21 additional cases of invasive aspergillosis following dengue (total n = 22; median age 36 years, 59% male) were identified, predominantly in Asia. Dengue IgM results were positive, and other tropical infections were excluded.

Most patients had severe dengue with multiorgan dysfunction. Fifteen cases (68%) had pulmonary involvement.
Research and practice implications
An autopsy revealed diffuse alveolar damage and extensive invasive aspergillosis involving the bronchi, pulmonary vasculature, and myocardium. Despite intensive care, the patient developed progressive respiratory failure, refractory shock, and acute kidney injury and died.

在哥伦比亚库库塔和洛斯帕蒂奥斯登革热流行社区实施保护涂层作为新型 媒介 控制工具的社区参与和操作经验教训。

Community participation and operational lessons from implementing a protective coating as a novel vector control tool in dengue-endemic neighborhoods of Cúcuta and Los Patios, Colombia.
作者： Susana Koo Chong, María Angelica Carrillo, Rocío Cárdenas Sánchez, Tatiana Rivera-Ramirez, Axel Kroeger, Rocio Cardenas Sanchez, Tatiana Rivera Ramírez
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景 控制伊蚊传播的疾病(登革热、寨卡、基孔肯雅热、黄热病)是热带和亚热带国家公共卫生的高度优先事项。采用新的 媒介 控制工具(例如保护涂层 (PC))需要了解影响其接受度和可行性的社会动态。本研究调查了参与哥伦比亚城市 媒介 控制项目的不同利益相关者的经验和观点,旨在评估社区接受度和运营可行性。获得的见解对于优化实施和衡量创新 媒介 控制策略的更广泛可行性非常重要。方法采用焦点小组讨论和深度访谈的方式,对社区领导(n=3)、社区志愿者(n=30)、媒介控制人员(n=7)、督导员(n=5)和社区居民(n=25)等70名关键利益相关者进行定性描述性研究。干预后收集数据,并使用开放编码的归纳主题内容分析进行分析。参与是自愿且保密的。结果保护涂层被认为是可行的并且普遍被社区所接受。促进因素包括社区领袖的参与、志愿者的坚定承诺以及机构之间的有效协调。通过适应性策略(包括调整工作时间表和加强沟通)识别并解决了信任问题、后勤限制和安全问题等挑战。结论 社区参与、有效沟通和适应性管理是成功实施保护涂层的关键,并有助于解决后勤挑战。吸取的经验教训为在拉丁美洲类似城市环境中推广新颖的 媒介 控制方法提供了有用的信息。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该审查审查了在哥伦比亚登革热流行的城市社区实施保护涂层(PC)作为控制伊蚊传播疾病的新型 媒介 工具的社区参与和操作可行性,重点关注利益相关者的接受度和实施挑战。
证据基础与方法
该研究采用定性描述性设计,在干预后对 70 名利益相关者(社区领导、志愿者、媒介 控制人员、主管和居民)进行焦点小组讨论和深入访谈,并通过开放编码的归纳主题内容分析进行分析。
共识与结论
该保护涂层被认为是可行的,并且普遍受到社区的广泛接受,其成功的关键因素包括社区领导的参与、志愿者的承诺和有效的机构协调。
争议与缺口
后勤限制、信任问题和安全问题被认为是挑战,尽管调整工作时间表和加强沟通等适应性策略有助于解决这些问题。

Community participation and operational lessons from implementing a protective coating as a novel vector control tool in dengue-endemic neighborhoods of Cúcuta and Los Patios, Colombia.

Authors: Susana Koo Chong, María Angelica Carrillo, Rocío Cárdenas Sánchez, Tatiana Rivera-Ramirez, Axel Kroeger, Rocio Cardenas Sanchez, Tatiana Rivera Ramírez
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background The control of Aedes-borne diseases (dengue, Zika, chikungunya, yellow fever) is a high public health priority in tropical and subtropical countries. The adoption of new vector control tools, such as protective coating (PC), requires understanding the social dynamics that influence their acceptance and feasibility. This study investigated the experiences and perspectives of diverse stakeholders involved in a vector control project in urban Colombia, with the aim of assessing community acceptance and operational feasibility. Insights gained are important for optimizing implementation and gauging the broader viability of innovative vector control strategies. Methods A qualitative descriptive study was conducted using focus group discussions and in-depth interviews with 70 key stakeholders, including community leaders ( n = 3), community volunteers ( n = 30), vector control staff ( n = 7), supervisors ( n = 5), and community residents ( n = 25). Data was collected after the intervention and analyzed using inductive thematic content analysis with open coding. Participation was voluntary and confidential. Results The protective coating was perceived as feasible and generally well accepted by the community. Facilitating factors included the involvement of community leaders, strong volunteer commitment, and effective coordination among institutions. Challenges such as trust issues, logistical constraints, and safety concerns were identified and addressed through adaptive strategies, including adjusted work schedules and strengthened communication. Conclusion Community involvement, effective communication, and adaptive management were key to the successful implementation of the protective coating and helped address logistical challenges. The lessons learned provide useful information for scaling up novel vector control approaches in similar urban settings in Latin America.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines community participation and operational feasibility of implementing a protective coating (PC) as a novel vector control tool for Aedes-borne diseases in dengue-endemic urban neighborhoods of Colombia, focusing on stakeholder acceptance and implementation challenges.
Evidence base and method
The study used a qualitative descriptive design with focus group discussions and in-depth interviews involving 70 stakeholders (community leaders, volunteers, vector control staff, supervisors, and residents) after the intervention, analyzed via inductive thematic content analysis with open coding.
Consensus and conclusions
The protective coating was perceived as feasible and generally well accepted by the community, with key success factors including community leader involvement, volunteer commitment, and effective institutional coordination.
Controversies and gaps
Logistical constraints, trust issues, and safety concerns were identified as challenges, though adaptive strategies such as adjusted work schedules and strengthened communication helped address them.
Research and practice implications
The lessons learned emphasize the importance of community involvement, effective communication, and adaptive management for scaling up novel vector control approaches in similar urban settings in Latin America.

鉴定登革热感染非侵入性生物标志物的代谢组学方法:系统评价

Metabolomic Approaches for the Identification of Non-Invasive Biomarkers in Dengue Infection: A Systematic Review
作者： Janni Gómez-Montoya, Daniela Martínez-Berrío, Nicole Espinosa-Bernal, Leidy Hurtado-Gómez, Díaz-Olmos Y, Elkin Navarro-Quiroz, Antonio Acosta Hoyos, Lisandro Pacheco-Lugo, Yirys Diaz-Olmos
摘要中文翻译登革热是一种主要的蚊媒病毒性疾病,其从自限性发热到出血性或休克综合征 (DHF/DSS) 的快速进展仍然难以使用传统的临床标志物进行预测。代谢组学为识别感染和严重程度的非侵入性生物标志物提供了一种有前途的途径,但证据仍然分散在生物体液和分析平台中。本系统综述综合了登革热感染的人类代谢组学研究,以表征迄今为止报道的生物体液、技术和代谢特征。根据 PRISMA 2020 指南,对 PubMed、ScienceDirect 和 Web of Science 进行了搜索,以查找将基于 NMR 或 MS 的代谢组学应用于人类登革热样本的研究,并根据需要健康对照或严重程度对照组的 PECO 标准进行筛选。在确定的 780 项记录中,17 项研究符合所有资格标准。大多数研究 (14/17) 分析了血液基质(血清、血浆或全血),其中三项研究分析了尿液;没有符合条件的研究检查粪便、唾液、眼泪、汗液或脑脊液。研究发现,随着疾病严重程度的增加,出现了三种趋同模式:血清素和相关色氨酸途径代谢物的消耗,甘油磷脂和溶血磷脂类别的紊乱,以及肝脏和脂质代谢应激标志物的升高(胆汁酸、VLDL/LDL变化、酰基肉碱)。血清素与 IFN-γ 联合使用,在 DHF 进展方面取得了最强的判别性能(AUC = 0.92),而尿烷烃和甜菜碱则成为与感染状态和糖尿病等合并症相关的候选非侵入性标志物。这些发现凸显了对血液采样的持续依赖,并且几乎完全缺乏探索替代的、更容易获得的生物流体的研究,这代表了未来登革热非侵入性生物标志物研究的明显差距。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注 登革病毒,专门研究登革热感染的非侵入性生物标志物的鉴定及其向登革出血热 (DHF) 和登革休克综合征 (DSS) 等严重形式的进展。核心问题是代谢组学方法是否可以为登革热疾病的严重程度提供可靠的、非侵入性的标志物。
证据基础与方法
该综述遵循 PRISMA 2020 指南,并搜索了 PubMed、ScienceDirect 和 Web of Science,以查找将基于 NMR 或 MS 的代谢组学应用于人类登革热样本的研究。资格标准需要健康对照或严重程度比较组,筛选后纳入了 17 项研究。
共识与结论
该综述确定了与登革热严重程度增加相关的三种趋同模式:血清素和相关色氨酸途径代谢物的消耗、甘油磷脂和溶血磷脂类别的紊乱以及肝脏和脂质代谢应激标志物的升高。5-羟色胺联合 IFN-γ 对进展为 DHF 表现出最强的区分性能,AUC 为 0.92。
争议与缺口
证据分散在生物流体和分析平台中,大多数研究都集中在血液基质上。没有符合条件的研究检查粪便、唾液、眼泪、汗液或脑脊液等替代生物流体,凸显了非侵入性生物标志物研究的空白。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应该探索用于非侵入性生物标志物开发的替代生物流体。此外,还需要进一步的研究来验证已确定的代谢特征及其在预测疾病严重程度方面的效用。

Metabolomic Approaches for the Identification of Non-Invasive Biomarkers in Dengue Infection: A Systematic Review

Authors: Janni Gómez-Montoya, Daniela Martínez-Berrío, Nicole Espinosa-Bernal, Leidy Hurtado-Gómez, Díaz-Olmos Y, Elkin Navarro-Quiroz, Antonio Acosta Hoyos, Lisandro Pacheco-Lugo, Yirys Diaz-Olmos
Original abstract (English)
Dengue is a major mosquito-borne viral disease whose rapid progression from self-limiting fever to hemorrhagic or shock syndrome (DHF/DSS) remains difficult to predict using conventional clinical markers. Metabolomics offers a promising avenue for identifying non-invasive biomarkers of infection and severity, but the evidence remains scattered across biofluids and analytical platforms. This systematic review synthesized human metabolomic studies of dengue infection to characterize the biofluids, techniques, and metabolic signatures reported to date. Following PRISMA 2020 guidelines, PubMed, ScienceDirect, and Web of Science were searched for studies applying NMR- or MS-based metabolomics to human dengue samples, screened against PECO criteria requiring a healthy-control or severity comparison group. Of 780 records identified, 17 studies met all eligibility criteria. Most studies (14/17) analyzed blood-derived matrices (serum, plasma, or whole blood), while three analyzed urine; no eligible study examined feces, saliva, tears, sweat, or cerebrospinal fluid. Across studies, three convergent patterns emerged with increasing disease severity: depletion of serotonin and related tryptophan-pathway metabolites, disturbance of glycerophospholipid and lysophospholipid classes, and elevation of markers of hepatic and lipid-metabolic stress (bile acids, VLDL/LDL changes, acylcarnitines). Serotonin combined with IFN-γ achieved the strongest discriminatory performance reported (AUC = 0.92) for progression to DHF, while urinary alkanes and betaine emerged as candidate non-invasive markers linked to infection status and comorbidities such as diabetes. These findings highlight a persistent reliance on blood-based sampling and a near-total absence of studies exploring alternative, more accessible biofluids, representing a clear gap for future non-invasive biomarker research in dengue.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on dengue virus, specifically examining the identification of non-invasive biomarkers for dengue infection and its progression to severe forms such as dengue hemorrhagic fever (DHF) and dengue shock syndrome (DSS). The central question is whether metabolomic approaches can provide reliable, non-invasive markers for dengue disease severity.
Evidence base and method
The review followed PRISMA 2020 guidelines and searched PubMed, ScienceDirect, and Web of Science for studies applying NMR- or MS-based metabolomics to human dengue samples. Eligibility criteria required a healthy-control or severity comparison group, and 17 studies were included after screening.
Consensus and conclusions
The review identified three convergent patterns associated with increasing dengue severity: depletion of serotonin and related tryptophan-pathway metabolites, disturbance of glycerophospholipid and lysophospholipid classes, and elevation of markers of hepatic and lipid-metabolic stress. Serotonin combined with IFN-γ showed the strongest discriminatory performance for progression to DHF, with an AUC of 0.92.
Controversies and gaps
The evidence is scattered across biofluids and analytical platforms, with most studies focusing on blood-derived matrices. No eligible study examined alternative biofluids such as feces, saliva, tears, sweat, or cerebrospinal fluid, highlighting a gap in non-invasive biomarker research.
Research and practice implications
Future research should explore alternative biofluids for non-invasive biomarker development. Additionally, further studies are needed to validate the identified metabolic signatures and their utility in predicting disease severity.

登革病毒 分子靶点和 抗病毒药物 药物开发:进展、挑战和治疗前景

Dengue Virus Molecular Targets and Antiviral Drug Development: Advances, Challenges and Therapeutic Perspectives
作者： Aryane Lucia da Silva Aguiar De Oliveira, José Arion Da Silva Moura, Pedro César de Souza, Marcelo Arthur Dias da Costa Santana, Matheus Vinicius Guimarães de Melo, Douglas Carvalho Francisco Viana, Marina Galdino Da Rocha Pitta, Isabella Luiza Ralph de Oliveira
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
Dengue is the most prevalent arboviral disease in tropical and subtropical regions, with over 13 million cases recorded in the Americas in 2024. The review focuses on the principal molecular targets of DENV, including structural proteins (C, prM/M and E), nonstructural proteins (NS1–NS5) and host factors, with an emphasis on the development of small-molecule antivirals and their progress in pre-clinical and clinical studies.
证据基础与方法
该综述是对 DENV 主要分子靶点的叙述性分析,包括结构蛋白(C、prM/M 和 E)、非结构蛋白(NS1-NS5)和 宿主 因子。它评估小分子 抗病毒药物 在临床前和临床研究中的进展,但不报告具体数据库、检索日期或资格标准。
共识与结论
由于高分辨率晶体结构和更高的血清型间保守性,NS2B-NS3 蛋白酶复合物和 NS5(RdRp 和 MTase 结构域)仍然是 DENV 探索最广泛的靶标。NS4B 已成为临床上最先进的靶点。
争议与缺口
该综述强调了关键的转化挑战,包括病毒血清型多样性、药代动力学限制、细胞毒性以及 治疗 相对于病毒动力学的启动时间。证据并未报告 宿主 定向疗法的有效性或 抗病毒药物 临床试验的具体结果。
科研与实践启示
合理药物设计、药物再利用、变构抑制和靶向蛋白质降解(PROTAC)方面的进展为有效的登革热治疗提供了新的视角。在直接作用的 抗病毒药物 中,AT-752 由于入组时病毒血症较低而停产,而 NITD-688 仍然是最先进的管道分子之一。宿主 定向疗法(包括阿那白滞素和巴瑞克替尼)正在接受 重症登革热 的临床研究。

Dengue Virus Molecular Targets and Antiviral Drug Development: Advances, Challenges and Therapeutic Perspectives

Authors: Aryane Lucia da Silva Aguiar De Oliveira, José Arion Da Silva Moura, Pedro César de Souza, Marcelo Arthur Dias da Costa Santana, Matheus Vinicius Guimarães de Melo, Douglas Carvalho Francisco Viana, Marina Galdino Da Rocha Pitta, Isabella Luiza Ralph de Oliveira
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Dengue is the most prevalent arboviral disease in tropical and subtropical regions, with over 13 million cases recorded in the Americas in 2024. Despite its enormous public health impact, no specific antiviral therapy is currently approved for dengue virus (DENV). In this context, this narrative review critically analyses the principal molecular targets of DENV, including structural proteins (C, prM/M and E), nonstructural proteins (NS1–NS5) and host factors, with emphasis on the development of small‐molecule antivirals and an evaluation of their progress in pre‐clinical and clinical studies. The NS2B–NS3 protease complex and NS5 (both RdRp and MTase domains) remain the most extensively explored targets, owing to the availability of high‐resolution crystal structures and higher inter‐serotype conservation. NS4B has emerged as the most clinically advanced target. Among direct‐acting antivirals, AT‐752 was also discontinued due to low viraemia at enrolment, while NITD‐688 remains among the most advanced pipeline molecules. Host‐directed therapies including anakinra and baricitinib are under clinical investigation for severe dengue. Key translational challenges include viral serotype diversity, pharmacokinetic limitations, cytotoxicity, and the timing of treatment initiation relative to viral kinetics. Advances in rational drug design, drug repurposing, allosteric inhibition, and targeted protein degradation (PROTACs) offer renewed perspectives for effective dengue therapeutics.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Dengue is the most prevalent arboviral disease in tropical and subtropical regions, with over 13 million cases recorded in the Americas in 2024. The review focuses on the principal molecular targets of DENV, including structural proteins (C, prM/M and E), nonstructural proteins (NS1–NS5) and host factors, with an emphasis on the development of small-molecule antivirals and their progress in pre-clinical and clinical studies.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative analysis of the principal molecular targets of DENV, including structural proteins (C, prM/M and E), nonstructural proteins (NS1–NS5) and host factors. It evaluates the progress of small-molecule antivirals in pre-clinical and clinical studies without reporting specific databases, search dates, or eligibility criteria.
Consensus and conclusions
The NS2B–NS3 protease complex and NS5 (both RdRp and MTase domains) remain the most extensively explored targets for DENV due to the availability of high-resolution crystal structures and higher inter-serotype conservation. NS4B has emerged as the most clinically advanced target.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights key translational challenges, including viral serotype diversity, pharmacokinetic limitations, cytotoxicity, and the timing of treatment initiation relative to viral kinetics. The evidence does not report on the effectiveness of host-directed therapies or the specific outcomes of clinical trials for antivirals.
Research and practice implications
Advances in rational drug design, drug repurposing, allosteric inhibition, and targeted protein degradation (PROTACs) offer renewed perspectives for effective dengue therapeutics. Among direct-acting antivirals, AT-752 was discontinued due to low viraemia at enrolment, while NITD-688 remains among the most advanced pipeline molecules. Host-directed therapies including anakinra and baricitinib are under clinical investigation for severe dengue.

孟加拉国 重症登革热 感染的 重症登革热:贝叶斯荟萃分析

Prevalence of Severe Dengue Infection in Bangladesh: A Bayesian Meta-analysis
作者： Sourav Roy, Md Ismail Hossain
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述检查了孟加拉国 重症登革热 感染的 重症登革热,重点关注进展至 重症登革热 的登革热病例比例,重症登革热 是一个以内出血和潜在死亡为特征的危及生命的阶段。
证据基础与方法
该综述从 2000 年 1 月到 2025 年 6 月对五个数据库(PubMed、Google Scholar、Cochrane Central、Science Direct 和 BanglaJOL)进行了系统文献检索,确定了 41 项横断面研究。使用 STROBE 检查表评估研究质量,并应用具有非信息性和信息性先验的贝叶斯随机效应模型来估计汇总的 患病率。
共识与结论
The pooled prevalence of severe dengue among dengue cases in Bangladesh was estimated at 33.9% (95% Credible Interval: 25.4, 42.0), with a between-study heterogeneity (τ2) of 0.074. Model diagnostics confirmed adequate mixing and convergence of MCMC chains, with no signs of autocorrelation.
争议与缺口
该综述没有报告纳入研究的地理或时间分布,限制了对 重症登革热 患病率 的区域或季节变化的了解。此外,对横断面研究的依赖可能会引入选择偏差,并且使用具有信息先验的贝叶斯模型可能会影响汇总估计。
科研与实践启示
重症登革热 的高汇总 监测 (33.9%) 突显了孟加拉国医疗保健系统的巨大负担,需要有针对性的公共卫生 监测 和资源分配。政策制定者应优先考虑采取干预措施来管理 重症登革热 案例,包括早期的 诊断 和 治疗 策略。

Prevalence of Severe Dengue Infection in Bangladesh: A Bayesian Meta-analysis

Authors: Sourav Roy, Md Ismail Hossain
Original abstract (English)
Severe dengue represents a critical and potentially life-threatening stage of dengue infection, often characterizes by internal bleeding which leads to death. Several studies existed on severe dengue but a meta-analysis can provide robust understanding of the epidemiology of the disease. This study aimed to estimate the pooled prevalence of dengue cases which developed to severe dengue in Bangladesh. A systematic literature search was conducted across five databases between January 2000 and June 2025: PubMed, Google Scholar, Cochrane Central, Science Direct, and BanglaJOL. After title and abstract screening and full text review, this study included 41 cross-sectional studies. The quality of included studies were assessed using the STROBE checklist. Bayesian random-effects model was applied to estimate the pooled prevalence, with both non-informative and informative priors. Further, model diagnostics including trace plots and autocorrelation checks were used to assess MCMC convergence. A Bayesian random-effects model using the strongly informative prior estimated a pooled prevalence of 33.9% (95% Credible Interval: 25.4, 42.0), and a between-study heterogeneity () of 0.074. Model diagnostics were further supported these findings by the evidence of adequate mixing and convergence, with no signs of autocorrelation in the MCMC chains. However, this study have found one-third of the dengue cases developed to severe dengue cases which indicates a serious burden for the healthcare system of the country. These findings have important implications for public health surveillance and can inform policymakers in planning and prioritizing interventions to manage severe dengue cases in Bangladesh. 74(2): 301–310, 2026 (July)
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the prevalence of severe dengue infection in Bangladesh, focusing on the proportion of dengue cases progressing to severe dengue, a life-threatening stage characterized by internal bleeding and potential fatality.
Evidence base and method
The review conducted a systematic literature search across five databases (PubMed, Google Scholar, Cochrane Central, Science Direct, and BanglaJOL) from January 2000 to June 2025, identifying 41 cross-sectional studies. Study quality was assessed using the STROBE checklist, and a Bayesian random-effects model with both non-informative and informative priors was applied to estimate pooled prevalence.
Consensus and conclusions
The pooled prevalence of severe dengue among dengue cases in Bangladesh was estimated at 33.9% (95% Credible Interval: 25.4, 42.0), with a between-study heterogeneity (τ2) of 0.074. Model diagnostics confirmed adequate mixing and convergence of MCMC chains, with no signs of autocorrelation.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not report the geographic or temporal distribution of included studies, limiting insights into regional or seasonal variations in severe dengue prevalence. Additionally, the reliance on cross-sectional studies may introduce selection bias, and the use of a Bayesian model with informative priors could influence pooled estimates.
Research and practice implications
The high pooled prevalence of severe dengue (33.9%) underscores a significant burden on Bangladesh's healthcare system, necessitating targeted public health surveillance and resource allocation. Policymakers should prioritize interventions to manage severe dengue cases, including early diagnosis and treatment strategies.

药用植物多靶点治疗登革热的传统知识和分子证据:综述

Traditional Knowledge and Molecular Evidence of Medicinal Plants for Multi-Target Dengue Therapy: A Review
作者： Mohammed Anas, Farhat Jahan
摘要中文翻译在气候变化、快速城市化和不断扩大的 媒介 栖息地的推动下,登革热 作为一项重大的全球公共卫生挑战继续加剧,而缺乏普遍安全有效的 抗病毒药物 疗法仍然是一个关键限制。与传统的单靶点 抗病毒药物 不同,基于植物的干预措施提供了多靶点治疗策略,能够同时抑制病毒复制、调节免疫失调、减少氧化应激、稳定内皮功能、增强血小板恢复,并有助于 媒介 的控制。据报道,多种植物化学物质,包括类黄酮、生物碱、单宁、多酚、多糖和生物活性酶,可与关键的登革热病毒蛋白(如 NS2B-NS3 蛋白酶和 NS5 RNA 依赖性 RNA 聚合酶)相互作用,同时还调节 宿主 炎症和免疫途径。将民族药理学数据与分子对接、网络药理学和临床前研究相结合,为传统使用的植物,特别是番木瓜、穿心莲和心叶青牛胆提供了机制验证。此外,对植物多样性、使用部位、植物化学类别和保护状况的分析突出了治疗前景和可持续性问题。尽管体外和计算机证据有希望,但临床转化仍然受到缺乏标准化配方、体内验证有限和大规模临床试验不足的限制。这篇综述全面整合了流行病学趋势、登革病毒 生物学、发病机制、传统知识和现代分子证据,以评估药用植物在登革热管理中的治疗潜力。总体而言,本次综述强调药用植物是下一代登革热疗法的基于证据的多目标候选药物,为减轻全球登革热负担提供了可持续且负担得起的途径。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述评估了药用植物在管理登革热方面的治疗潜力,重点关注 登革病毒 1-4 和登革热疾病。它探索传统知识与分子证据的整合,以确定登革热治疗的多目标干预措施。
证据基础与方法
该综述将民族药理学数据与分子对接、网络药理学和临床前研究相结合。它不报告具体的数据库、检索日期或纳入研究的资格标准。
共识与结论
据报道,多种植物化学物质,包括类黄酮、生物碱、单宁、多酚、多糖和生物活性酶,可与关键的登革热病毒蛋白(如 NS2B-NS3 蛋白酶和 NS5 RNA 依赖性 RNA 聚合酶)相互作用,同时还调节 宿主 炎症和免疫途径。
争议与缺口
尽管体外和计算机证据有希望,但临床转化仍然受到缺乏标准化配方、体内验证有限和大规模临床试验不足的限制。
科研与实践启示
对植物多样性、使用部位、植物化学类别和保护状况的分析突出了治疗前景和可持续性问题,表明需要对标准化、体内验证和生态影响进行进一步研究。

Traditional Knowledge and Molecular Evidence of Medicinal Plants for Multi-Target Dengue Therapy: A Review

Authors: Mohammed Anas, Farhat Jahan
Original abstract (English)
Dengue fever continues to intensify as a major global public health challenge, driven by climate change, rapid urbanization, and expanding vector habitats, while the absence of a universally safe and effective antiviral therapy remains a critical limitation. Unlike conventional single-target antivirals, plant-based interventions offer a multi-target therapeutic strategy capable of simultaneously inhibiting viral replication, modulating immune dysregulation, reducing oxidative stress, stabilizing endothelial function, enhancing platelet recovery, and contributing to vector control. A wide range of phytochemicals, including flavonoids, alkaloids, tannins, polyphenols, polysaccharides, and bioactive enzymes, have been reported to interact with key dengue viral proteins such as NS2B-NS3 protease and NS5 RNA-dependent RNA polymerase, while also regulating host inflammatory and immunological pathways. Integration of ethnopharmacological data with molecular docking, network pharmacology, and preclinical studies provides mechanistic validation for traditionally used plants, particularly Carica papaya, Andrographis paniculata, and Tinospora cordifolia. Additionally, analysis of plant diversity, parts used, phytochemical classes, and conservation status highlights both therapeutic promise and sustainability concerns. Despite promising in-vitro and in-silico evidence, clinical translation remains constrained by lack of standardized formulations, limited in-vivo validation, and insufficient large-scale clinical trials. This review comprehensively integrates epidemiological trends, dengue virus biology, pathogenesis, traditional knowledge, and modern molecular evidence to evaluate the therapeutic potential of medicinal plants in dengue management. Overall, this review underscores medicinal plants as evidence-based, multi-target candidates for next-generation dengue therapeutics, offering a sustainable and affordable pathway to mitigate the escalating global dengue burden.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review evaluates the therapeutic potential of medicinal plants in managing dengue, focusing on dengue viruses 1–4 and dengue disease. It explores the integration of traditional knowledge with molecular evidence to identify multi-target interventions for dengue therapy.
Evidence base and method
The review integrates ethnopharmacological data with molecular docking, network pharmacology, and preclinical studies. It does not report specific databases, search dates, or eligibility criteria for included studies.
Consensus and conclusions
A wide range of phytochemicals, including flavonoids, alkaloids, tannins, polyphenols, polysaccharides, and bioactive enzymes, have been reported to interact with key dengue viral proteins such as NS2B-NS3 protease and NS5 RNA-dependent RNA polymerase, while also regulating host inflammatory and immunological pathways.
Controversies and gaps
Despite promising in-vitro and in-silico evidence, clinical translation remains constrained by lack of standardized formulations, limited in-vivo validation, and insufficient large-scale clinical trials.
Research and practice implications
Analysis of plant diversity, parts used, phytochemical classes, and conservation status highlights both therapeutic promise and sustainability concerns, suggesting the need for further research on standardization, in-vivo validation, and ecological impact.

维生素 D 缺乏症和 重症登革热 发烧:系统回顾和荟萃分析

Vitamin D Deficiency and Severe Dengue Fever: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis
作者： Chuchun Zhuang, Xinxiaodi Yan, Yufeng Liang, Lihua Zhang, Lian Cai, Yukun Lin, Xiaowen Feng, Huacheng Yan
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述调查了维生素 D 缺乏与 重症登革热 发烧之间的关联,重点关注 重症登革热 1-4 和登革热疾病。其目的是确定维生素 D 缺乏是否是 重症登革热 结果的危险因素。
证据基础与方法
该综述系统地检索了比较严重和非严重登革热病例之间维生素 D 水平的观察性研究。进行了随机效应荟萃分析,并进行了亚组和敏感性分析,以评估研究结果的异质性和稳健性。
共识与结论
The pooled odds ratio for severe dengue with vitamin D deficiency was 1.77 (95% CI: 0.75–4.16, P = 0.194), indicating no statistically significant association overall. However, subgroup analysis revealed a significant association in children (OR: 51.20, 95% CI: 3.12–842.95).
争议与缺口
研究结果不一致,各研究之间存在显着的异质性 (I 2 = 61.6%)。排除异常值减少了异质性,但仍然留下了一些不确定性。该评论强调需要更大的样本量和前瞻性研究来确认关联性并建立因果关系。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应优先考虑前瞻性研究,以评估因果关系并评估补充维生素 D 对高危人群(尤其是儿童)的潜在益处,以更好地了解其在 重症登革热 结果中的作用。

Vitamin D Deficiency and Severe Dengue Fever: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis

Authors: Chuchun Zhuang, Xinxiaodi Yan, Yufeng Liang, Lihua Zhang, Lian Cai, Yukun Lin, Xiaowen Feng, Huacheng Yan
Original abstract (English)
Vitamin D affects immune function and possibly, dengue severity, but evidence is inconsistent. Observational studies comparing vitamin D levels between severe and nonsevere dengue were systematically searched. A random-effects meta-analysis was conducted. Heterogeneity was quantified using I 2 . Subgroup and sensitivity analyses were performed. Six studies with 1,130 participants were included. The pooled odds ratio (OR) for severe dengue with vitamin D deficiency was 1.77 (95% CI: 0.75–4.16, P = 0.194) with substantial heterogeneity ( I 2 = 61.6%). A subgroup analysis showed a significant association in children (OR: 51.20, 95% CI: 3.12–842.95). Excluding an outlier yielded a significant fixed-effect OR of 1.67 (95% CI: 1.04–2.67, P = 0.034) with reduced heterogeneity ( I 2 = 46.1%). Vitamin D deficiency might be linked to higher odds of severe dengue, especially in children, but confirmation requires larger sample sizes. Establishing causality and assessing vitamin D supplementation in high-risk groups require prospective studies.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review investigates the association between vitamin D deficiency and severe dengue fever, focusing on dengue viruses 1–4 and dengue disease. It aims to determine whether vitamin D deficiency is a risk factor for severe dengue outcomes.
Evidence base and method
The review systematically searched observational studies comparing vitamin D levels between severe and nonsevere dengue cases. A random-effects meta-analysis was conducted, and subgroup and sensitivity analyses were performed to assess heterogeneity and robustness of findings.
Consensus and conclusions
The pooled odds ratio for severe dengue with vitamin D deficiency was 1.77 (95% CI: 0.75–4.16, P = 0.194), indicating no statistically significant association overall. However, subgroup analysis revealed a significant association in children (OR: 51.20, 95% CI: 3.12–842.95).
Controversies and gaps
The findings are inconsistent, with substantial heterogeneity (I 2 = 61.6%) across studies. Excluding an outlier reduced heterogeneity but still left some uncertainty. The review highlights the need for larger sample sizes and prospective studies to confirm the association and establish causality.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritize prospective studies to assess causality and evaluate the potential benefits of vitamin D supplementation in high-risk groups, particularly children, to better understand its role in severe dengue outcomes.

Pinellas County increases mosquito control to limit spread of Dengue Fever - pinellas.gov
新闻简报据 2026 年 9 月 1 日报道,佛罗里达州皮内拉斯县加大了蚊虫控制力度,以限制 登革热 的传播。该县处于蚊媒疾病警报之下,强调了疾病 传播 的潜在风险。虽然证据中没有提供具体病例或死亡人数,但这一行动表明对威胁的积极响应。这些措施包括加强 监测 和社区参与,以减少蚊子滋生地。该县的公共卫生战略旨在通过针对蚊子活动频繁的地区来预防 暴发。这项应对措施正在进行中,也是保护居民免受蚊媒疾病侵害的更广泛举措的一部分。

佛罗里达州皮尼拉斯县。受影响的人群包括该县的居民,特别是蚊子活动频繁地区的居民。
事件
皮内拉斯县加大了蚊虫控制力度,以限制 登革热 的传播。皮尼拉斯县政府报告了这一行动,作为其公共卫生举措的一部分。

应对、状态与不确定性
针对登革热风险,该县正在积极实施灭蚊措施。应对措施正在进行中,并且是更广泛的公共卫生战略的一部分。证据没有具体说明调查的确切状态或任何确诊病例。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Pinellas County increases mosquito control to limit spread of Dengue Fever - pinellas.gov

News Brief
Pinellas County, Florida, has increased mosquito control efforts to limit the spread of Dengue Fever, as reported on September 1, 2026. The county is under a Mosquito-Borne Illness Alert, highlighting the potential risk of disease transmission. While no specific case or death counts are provided in the evidence, the action indicates a proactive response to the threat. The measures include enhanced surveillance and community engagement to reduce mosquito breeding sites. The county's public health strategy aims to prevent outbreaks by targeting areas with high mosquito activity. The response is ongoing and part of a broader initiative to protect residents from mosquito-borne illnesses.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

Monoclonal antibody shows promise against dengue viremia, fever in phase 2 trial

Monoclonal antibody shows promise against dengue viremia, fever in phase 2 trial
新闻简报A phase 2 clinical trial of an experimental monoclonal antibody (Dengue-mAb) for dengue virus showed promising results, with rapid clearance of viremia and fever within 24 hours at all dose levels compared to a placebo. The study, published in JAMA Network Open, involved 250 adults with dengue and a history of fever onset within 48 hours, with 50 participants assigned to each of four dose levels and a placebo group. The primary outcomes were reduction in viremia at 24 hours and serious adverse events. Dengue-mAb was safe and well-tolerated, with no causally related serious adverse events recorded. The trial demonstrated a therapeutic effect, with fever clearance ranging from 92.9% to 100% in the Dengue-mAb groups versus 58.3% in the placebo group. The authors noted that this is the first evidence of a preliminary therapeutic effect in patients with dengue. The study also highlighted the global impact of dengue virus, which affects over 200 million people annually, and the rising incidence of severe disease. While there are three licensed dengue vaccines, no licensed antiviral drugs exist, underscoring the need for further research and improvements in case management.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该试验于上周报道,该研究的随访期跨越多年,第一年观察到的风险最高。
地点与对象
该试验涉及 250 名患有登革热且有 48 小时内发烧史的成年人,分为四个剂量水平和一个安慰剂组。该研究还引用了有关 登革病毒 影响的更广泛的全球数据。
事件
在一项 2 期临床试验中测试了一种实验性单克隆抗体 (Dengue-mAb) 对 登革病毒 的治疗效果,结果显示,与安慰剂相比,所有剂量水平的病毒血症和发烧都能在 24 小时内快速清除。该研究还指出,没有记录到因果相关的严重不良事件。
规模、影响与风险
The trial demonstrated a therapeutic effect in patients with dengue, with fever clearance ranging from 92.9% to 100% in the Dengue-mAb groups versus 58.3% in the placebo group. The study also highlighted the global impact of dengue virus, affecting over 200 million people annually, and the rising incidence of severe disease.
应对、状态与不确定性
该试验证明了登革热单克隆抗体的安全性及其对登革热病毒血症和发烧的治疗作用。研究结果被描述为初步的,作者认为 重症登革热 的病例管理仍然需要改进。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Monoclonal antibody shows promise against dengue viremia, fever in phase 2 trial

News Brief
A phase 2 clinical trial of an experimental monoclonal antibody (Dengue-mAb) for dengue virus showed promising results, with rapid clearance of viremia and fever within 24 hours at all dose levels compared to a placebo. The study, published in JAMA Network Open, involved 250 adults with dengue and a history of fever onset within 48 hours, with 50 participants assigned to each of four dose levels and a placebo group. Skip to main content Main navigation News Antimicrobial Resistance COVID-19 Chronic Wasting Disease Influenza Public Health Alerts All Topics A-Z Coronavirus Vaccines Roadmap Influenza Vaccines Roadmap Roadmaps for Priority Pathogens Vaccine Integrity Project CIDRAP Leadership Forum . . Topics & Projects Antimicrobial Stewardship Chronic Wasting Disease Osterholm Update Podcasts Newsletters About CIDRAP CIDRAP in the News Our Staff Our Director Osterholm in the Press Shop Merchandise Contact Us About Search CLOSE Search Support Menu Monoclonal antibody shows promise against dengue viremia, fever in phase 2 trial News brief Chris Dall, MA Topics Dengue Share Copied to clipboard Monica R / Flickr cc An experimental monoclonal antibody (mAb) for dengue virus was safe and well-tolerated and provided evidence of a therapeutic effect in a phase 2 clinical trial, researchers reported last week in JAMA Network Open. In the randomized, placebo-controlled trial, a team led by investigators from Serum Institute of India assigned 250 adults with dengue and a history of fever onset within 48 hours to receive four different dose levels of Dengue-mAb (3, 5, 7, or 9 milligrams per kilogram [mg/kg]) or a placebo, with 50 participants assigned to each group. The primary outcomes were reduction in viremia at 24 hours and causally related serious adverse events (SAEs). Dengue-mAb is a human-engineered, recombinant monoclonal antibody that has neutralized all four serotypes of dengue virus in animal studies and demonstrated safety in a phase 1 trial in healthy adults. Dengue virus affects more than 200 million people each year and is expanding into new areas amid climate change and rapid urbanization. In addition, the incidence of severe disease from the fast-spreading mosquito-borne virus is rising. While there are three currently licensed vaccines for dengue, there are no licensed antiviral drugs. “Improvements in case management to reduce the risk of severe dengue are still needed,” the investigators wrote. Rapid clearance of viremia, fever Treatment with Dengue-mAb demonstrated a rapid clearance of dengue viremia and fever within 24 hours at all dose levels compared with placebo. Participants with detectable viremia at baseline were negative for dengue virus by eight hours when treated with the 5- to 9-mg/kg dose of Dengue-mAb, compared with 72 hours with placebo. At 24 hours, fever clearance ranged from 92.9% to 100% in the Dengue-mAb groups, compared with 58.3% in the placebo group. This is, to our knowledge, the first evidence of a preliminary therapeutic effect in patients with dengue. No causally related SAEs were recorded. “This first-in-patient phase 2 randomized clinical trial demonstrated the safety of Dengue-mAb and its action on the clearance of dengue virus and fever,” the authors wrote. “This is, to our knowledge, the first evidence of a preliminary therapeutic effect in patients with dengue.” HPV infection may confer slightly higher risk of new-onset heart disease and death News brief Mary Van Beusekom, MS Topics Human Papillomavirus (HPV) Chinnapong / iStock Women infected with human papillomavirus (HPV) are at higher risk for new-onset cardiovascular disease (CVD) and death than their uninfected peers, a Karolinska Institutet–led research team suggests. For the observational study, published yesterday in PLOS Medicine, the researchers compared the CVD diagnoses of 497,445 HPV-infected women in Swedish national registries with those of roughly 2.5 million uninfected controls. They also compared CVD status among 143,787 infected women and their 174,637 unexposed full siblings. The authors note that most previous observational research was limited to specific populations and didn’t adequately consider familial factors. HPV infection is well known to cause cervical cancer, but emerging evidence suggests that it may also contribute to CVD. Sibling comparison yields similar findings New-onset CVD was identified in 28,793 and 128,373 HPV-infected and uninfected women, respectively, for respective incidences of 8.27 and 7.93 per 1,000 person-years. In total, 1,221 and 4,941 women with and without HPV infection died of CVD, for respective death rates of 33.7 and 29.4 per 100,000 person-years. The risks of CVD and death were greatest within the first year of follow-up, then waned. The findings suggested that clinicians should be aware of a slightly elevated CVD risk in women with HPV, especially during the first year following an infection. The risk of new-onset CVD was 7% higher in HPV-infected women than in their uninfected counterparts, for an absolute rate difference of 0.53 cases per 1,000 person-years (population-attributable fraction, 1.16%). The risk of death was 25% higher in infected women than in unexposed women, for an absolute rate difference of 6.71 cases per 100,000 person-years (population-attributable fraction, 4.12%). In the first year of follow-up, the risks of incident CVD and related death were 43% and 86% higher, respectively, in women diagnosed as having HPV. But thereafter, the excess risk fell to 2% for new-onset CVD and 21% for related death. In the sibling comparison, HPV-infected women had a 5% greater risk of incident CVD and a 25% higher risk of death than their uninfected siblings. “The findings suggested that clinicians should be aware of a slightly elevated CVD risk in women with HPV, especially during the first year following an infection,” the authors concluded. “However, given the small absolute difference, women should not be unduly concerned.” In case you missed it Previous Next Rotavirus vaccine shows strong global results in preschoolers Antiviral drug remdesivir may cut risk of death in adults with severe COVID and kidney or liver disease Quick takes: Schools resume in DR Congo; sprouts, mangos recalled; 2 H9N2 avian flu cases in China Ebola death toll approaches 3,000, as case-fatality rate hovers around 50% Annenberg poll: Americans trust their doctors when it comes to public health, but not FDA, CDC Long COVID not linked to preterm birth but may be to pregnancy-related high blood pressure disorders BARDA places first order for cefiderocol for resistant infections, biothreats Quick takes: Infant botulism, New World screwworm preventative, ‘airport malaria’ in Germany, new polio cases in Africa This week's top reads Measles-related deaths in Pennsylvania mark first 2 US fatalities this year Both people were unvaccinated and lived in Lancaster County, where a large measles outbreak started earlier this year. View five news elements
Time
The trial was reported last week, and the study's follow-up period spanned multiple years, with the highest risks observed within the first year.
Location and population
The trial involved 250 adults with dengue and a history of fever onset within 48 hours, distributed across four dose levels and a placebo group. The study also referenced broader global data on dengue virus impact.
Event
An experimental monoclonal antibody (Dengue-mAb) was tested in a phase 2 clinical trial for its therapeutic effect against dengue virus, showing rapid clearance of viremia and fever within 24 hours at all dose levels compared to placebo. The study also noted that no causally related serious adverse events were recorded.
Scale, impact and risk
The trial demonstrated a therapeutic effect in patients with dengue, with fever clearance ranging from 92.9% to 100% in the Dengue-mAb groups versus 58.3% in the placebo group. The study also highlighted the global impact of dengue virus, affecting over 200 million people annually, and the rising incidence of severe disease.
Response, status and uncertainty
The trial showed the safety of Dengue-mAb and its therapeutic effect on dengue viremia and fever. The findings are described as preliminary, and the authors suggest that improvements in case management for severe dengue are still needed.

Dengue Cases Across the Americas Fell 60 Percent This Year and Travelers Still Need to Watch Symptoms - Medical Daily

News Brief
A new report from Medical Daily highlights a 60% decline in dengue cases across the Americas this year, yet still urges travelers to remain alert for dengue-like symptoms. The article frames the drop as preliminary and under continued monitoring, without specifying national totals, death counts, or exposure figures. It emphasizes vigilance for travelers returning from affected regions, noting that symptoms can mimic other febrile illnesses. No official public-health measures or detailed epidemiological breakdowns are included in the summary.

登革热 暴发 袭击肯尼亚,据报道 5 人死亡 - the-star.co.ke

Dengue outbreak now hits Kenya, 5 deaths reported - the-star.co.ke

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue outbreak now hits Kenya, 5 deaths reported - the-star.co.ke

News Brief
A dengue fever outbreak has been reported in Kenya, specifically in Garissa and Wajir counties, with a total of 1,678 cases and five suspected deaths. Garissa Township and Hagadera have been the most affected areas, with 251 and 4 cases respectively. The Ministry of Health has confirmed the outbreak and is collaborating with county governments and partners to contain it. Public Health Principal Secretary Mary Muthoni emphasized the importance of sustained surveillance to verify the interruption of transmission. She also urged residents to eliminate stagnant water sources and take preventive measures against mosquito bites. The outbreak has prompted health authorities to strengthen measures, including laboratory confirmation and vector control, to prevent further spread. The case-fatality rate is less than one percent in most affected countries with timely medical care, according to the World Health Organization.
Original source text (English)
Aedes Mosquito. The Ministry of Health has moved to contain a dengue fever outbreak in Garissa and Wajir after the two counties reported 1,678 cases and five suspected deaths. Garissa is at the centre of the outbreak, with 1,583 cumulative reported cases as of August 26, according to the latest Ministry update. Wajir has recorded 95 laboratory-confirmed cases. All five suspected deaths were reported in Garissa, with four occurring in Garissa Township and one in Hagadera. Public Health Principal Secretary Mary Muthoni said the government was closely monitoring the situation and had strengthened measures to stop further transmission. “As at August26, 2026, Garissa county had recorded 1,583 cumulative reported dengue cases, while Wajir county had recorded 95 laboratory-confirmed cases, bringing the total to 1,678 cases,” she said. The highest number of cases in Garissa has been reported in Dadaab, which accounts for 1,276 cases. Garissa Township has recorded 251, while Fafi has reported 56 cases. In Wajir, Wajir East accounts for 85 cases, or 89.5 per cent, of the county's confirmed cases. The other 10 cases are spread across Eldas, Wajir West, Wajir North, Wajir South, Tarbaj and Khorof Harar. Children and adolescents aged between six months and 15 years account for 46 cases, or 48.4 per cent, of Wajir's confirmed infections. Muthoni said the ministry was working with county governments and other partners to contain the disease. “Samples from suspected dengue cases are being referred to the national reference laboratory for confirmation, while severe cases and suspected deaths are being investigated and classified as a priority,” she said. Dengue is a viral infection spread mainly through the bite of infected Aedes mosquitoes. The mosquitoes commonly live around homes and can breed in small amounts of stagnant water in containers, tyres, bottles and other objects. Unlike malaria mosquitoes, Aedes mosquitoes are active mainly during the day, although they can also bite at night. A mosquito becomes infected after biting a person carrying the dengue virus and can then transmit the virus when it bites another person, Kenya Medical Research Institute says. Most people infected with dengue either have no symptoms or develop a mild illness. Common signs include high fever, severe headache, pain behind the eyes, muscle and joint pain, nausea, vomiting and skin rash. Most people recover within one to two weeks, but dengue can sometimes become dangerous. About one in 20 people who become sick with dengue develops severe dengue, according to the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. Severe disease can cause internal bleeding, shock and failure of vital organs. Deaths can be greatly reduced when patients receive timely medical care, the World Health Organization (WHO) says. Proper hospital management has reduced the dengue case-fatality rate to less than one per cent in most affected countries. Muthoni urged residents to help break the mosquito's breeding cycle by removing stagnant water around homes and other buildings. “Households, institutions and communities should empty, scrub and tightly cover water-storage containers at least once each week, dispose of bottles, tins and tyres that can collect water, and clear blocked drains,” she said. Residents have also been advised to use mosquito repellent, wear long clothing and use window and door screens, especially during the day. People with fever, severe headache, pain behind the eyes, body or joint pains, nausea, vomiting or rash should seek medical care. Urgent treatment is needed for severe abdominal pain, persistent vomiting, bleeding, breathing difficulty, extreme weakness, restlessness or rapid deterioration, which can signal severe dengue, Muthoni said. The ministry advised patients to use paracetamol for fever as directed by a health professional and avoid aspirin or ibuprofen because these medicines can increase the risk of bleeding. Muthoni said the government would continue monitoring the counties despite no new cases being reported in the latest county updates. “Although no new cases have been reported since the latest county updates, sustained surveillance remains essential before interruption of transmission can be verified,” she said. “The ministry will continue working with county governments, health facilities, community health teams and partners to maintain clinical readiness, laboratory confirmation, vector control and timely public information.”
View five news elements
Time
The outbreak was reported as of August 26, 2026, with the latest updates on August 29, 2026. The suspected deaths were reported in Garissa and Wajir, with no new cases reported since the latest county updates.
Location and population
The outbreak is centered in Garissa and Wajir counties, Kenya. Garissa Township and Hagadera are specific areas within Garissa. In Wajir, Wajir East accounts for 89.5% of confirmed cases, and children and adolescents aged six months to 15 years account for 48.4% of confirmed infections.
Event
A dengue fever outbreak has been reported in Garissa and Wajir counties, Kenya, with 1,678 cumulative cases and five suspected deaths. The Ministry of Health has confirmed the outbreak and is working with county governments and partners to contain it.
Scale, impact and risk
Garissa has recorded 1,583 cumulative reported cases, while Wajir has 95 laboratory-confirmed cases. All five suspected deaths were in Garissa, with four in Garissa Township and one in Hagadera. The case-fatality rate is less than one percent in most affected countries with proper hospital management.
Response, status and uncertainty
The government is closely monitoring the situation, strengthening measures to stop transmission, and working with county governments, health facilities, and partners. Sustained surveillance is ongoing to verify the interruption of transmission, and contact tracing and vector control are being implemented.

越南南部登革热病例激增,医院为8月至11月高峰做好准备 - vietnamnews.vn

Dengue cases surge in southern Việt Nam, hospitals brace for August-November peak - vietnamnews.vn
新闻简报Dengue cases have surged in southern Việt Nam, with over 56,000 infections recorded in the first seven months of 2026, surpassing five-year averages. This increase, which is 34 per cent higher than the same period in 2025, has prompted hospitals to prepare for a peak season from August to November. The Hospital for Tropical Diseases in HCM City reported 31,098 inpatient treatments, with two-thirds of patients showing warning signs and 17 per cent-20 per cent classified as severe cases. Ten deaths were recorded, according to a report by the Pasteur Institute. Hospitals are implementing internal reporting systems using Zalo to track new cases and manage patient flow. Health authorities have urged hospitals to ensure adequate supplies of medicines and diagnostic materials, as well as maintain intensive-care capacity, including ventilators and isolation beds. The health ministry has noted that dengue outbreaks are becoming more frequent, with a cycle now appearing to be around three to four years. Dengue is expected to coincide with other infectious diseases, such as hand, foot, and mouth disease, requiring heightened preparedness.

The number of dengue cases increased by 34 per cent compared to the same period last year, with 10 deaths reported. The hospital recorded 31,098 inpatient treatments, with two-thirds of patients showing warning signs and 17 per cent-20 per cent classified as severe cases.

Dengue cases surge in southern Việt Nam, hospitals brace for August-November peak - vietnamnews.vn

News Brief
Dengue cases have surged in southern Việt Nam, with over 56,000 infections recorded in the first seven months of 2026, surpassing five-year averages. This increase, which is 34 per cent higher than the same period in 2025, has prompted hospitals to prepare for a peak season from August to November. Original source text (English)
Thursday, September 03 2026 Vietnam Law & Legal Forum Magazine Bizhub Ovietnam About Us Contact Us Việt Nam News Politics & Law Economy Sci-Tech Society Life & Style Sports Environment Opinion World E-Paper Brandinfo Sunday/Weekend Features Travel Politics & Law Society Economy Business Beat Talking Shop Environment Talk Around Town Opinion Outlook Op-Ed In the Spotlight Life & Style Expat Corner nom-nom World Sports Video Photo E-Paper Focus Bringing Resolutions to Life Viet Nam New Era Hanoi Investment Promotion Land Law Insights Hanoi Tourism Ho Chi Minh City in focus Việt Nam Insight IUU Combat 500-day campaign Happy National Day Home Society Dengue cases surge in southern Việt Nam, hospitals brace for August-November peak August 28, 2026 - 16:18 Your browser does not support the audio element. Dengue infections in southern Việt Nam have risen sharply this year, with more than 56,000 cases recorded in the first seven months and mosquito-borne disease indicators above five-year averages, prompting hospitals to prepare for a peak in admissions from August to November. Children are treated for dengue fever at the Department of Intensive Care and Anti-Poison at Children Hospital 1 in HCM City. — VNA/VNS Photo HCM CITY — Dengue infections in southern Việt Nam have risen sharply this year, with more than 56,000 cases recorded in the first seven months and mosquito-borne disease indicators above five-year averages, prompting hospitals to prepare for a peak in admissions from August to November. The number of dengue cases in the region increased 34 per cent from the same period last year, with 10 deaths, according to a report by the Pasteur Institute in HCM City. Vector surveillance indicators, which reflect the presence of mosquitoes that transmit the dengue virus, have also exceeded five-year average levels, signalling a higher risk of transmission. At the HCM City Hospital for Tropical Diseases, a referral centre for severe infectious diseases in southern Việt Nam, dengue admissions have risen rapidly, with more than 120 patients treated as inpatients each day recently and as many as 150 on some days. “This year is considered one of the years when dengue reaches a peak at the hospital,” said Dr Nguyễn Thành Dũng, the hospital’s director. Dengue outbreaks tend to follow a cyclical pattern, with major surges occurring every few years. The health ministry has previously said the cycle appears to have shortened to around three to four years. This year's increase follows trends seen in 2022 and 2025, the hospital said. From January 2022 to June 2026, the Hospital for Tropical Diseases recorded about 105,000 inpatient treatments and 1.9 million outpatient visits. Dengue accounted for 31,098 inpatient treatments during the period, with about two-thirds of patients showing warning signs and 17 per cent-20 per cent classified as severe cases. The hospital recorded eight dengue-related deaths, equivalent to 0.03 per cent of treated cases, as well as 35 severe cases in which patients' families requested discharge against medical advice. To manage the expected increase in patients, the hospital has established an internal reporting system using the Zalo messaging platform, allowing departments to continuously update figures on new cases, current inpatients and patients in shock or with severe symptoms. The system enables doctors on duty to quickly assess the situation and arrange monitoring, consultations and emergency interventions, Dũng said. Việt Nam’s health sector is also preparing for the possibility of dengue coinciding with other infectious diseases, including hand, foot and mouth disease. Health authorities have urged hospitals to prepare resources ahead of the peak season, focusing on early screening, disease classification and timely specialist consultations. Hospitals have been advised to ensure adequate supplies of medicines and diagnostic materials while maintaining intensive-care capacity, including ventilators, dialysis equipment, extracorporeal membrane oxygenation (ECMO) systems and isolation beds. The Hospital for Tropical Diseases is also strengthening data sharing with the HCM City Centre for Disease Control and the Pasteur Institute to improve disease surveillance and early warnings. Dũng said the health sector was no longer in a period between major infectious disease outbreaks, as diseases were continuing to re-emerge or increase at different times. He also warned that reduced exposure to some infectious diseases during periods of low transmission could leave communities with lower immunity, potentially allowing diseases to spread more rapidly when they return. — VNS Tags close Advertisements custom_chain_english_zodiac[webstory]-new-20260831-08:01 arrow_forward_ios Read more CANCEL NEXT VIDEO play_arrow volume_mute AD PausePlay % buffered00:00 00:01 01:31 UnmuteMute Play E-paper E-paper Subscribe Print Advertisement Online Advertisement Latest Most read Midea to Showcase SpaceMaster Series with Graphene Technology at IFA 2026 1. More Than 40 Locally Acquired Dengue Cases Reported in Florida; FMCA and Mosquito Control Professionals to Help Prevent Spread - PR Newswire

B routine priorityPublic-health news

More Than 40 Locally Acquired Dengue Cases Reported in Florida; FMCA and Mosquito Control Professionals to Help Prevent Spread - PR Newswire

News Brief
On August 31, 2026, more than 40 locally acquired dengue cases were reported in Florida, with over 30 in Hillsborough County, seven in Miami-Dade County, three in Pinellas County, and one in Palm Beach County. The Florida Mosquito Control Association (FMCA) and mosquito control professionals are working to prevent the spread of mosquito-borne illnesses. The Florida Department of Health issued mosquito-borne illness alerts in Hillsborough, Miami-Dade, and Pinellas counties, and an advisory in Palm Beach County. These cases highlight the need for continued mosquito surveillance and control efforts in the region. The situation is being actively managed by local health authorities and mosquito control professionals.
Original source text (English)

Inquiries Editorial Bureaus Partnerships Media Inquiries Worldwide Offices Hamburger menu Send a Release ALL CONTACT INFO Contact Us 888-776-0942 from 8 AM - 10 PM ET More Than 40 Locally Acquired Dengue Cases Reported in Florida; FMCA and Mosquito Control Professionals to Help Prevent Spread News provided by Florida Mosquito Control Association Aug 31, 2026, 10:15 ET Share this article Share toX Share this article Share toX Tallahassee, Fla., Aug. 31, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- With more than 40 confirmed human cases of locally-acquired dengue fever in the state – including more than 30 in the Tampa Bay area - the Florida Mosquito Control Association (FMCA) is urging Floridians to take precautions and help mosquito control professionals across the state reduce the risk of mosquito borne illnesses. The confirmed cases, which prompted the Florida Department of Health in Hillsborough County to issue a mosquito-borne illness alert, underscore the importance of Florida's year-round mosquito surveillance, prevention and control efforts. The locally acquired cases reported in Florida this year include 30 in Hillsborough County, seven in Miami-Dade County, three in Pinellas County and one in Palm Beach County. Hillsborough, Miami-Dade and Pinellas counties are currently under mosquito-borne illness alerts, while Palm Beach County is under a mosquito-borne illness advisory. In Hillsborough County, 10 mosquito pools have also tested positive for dengue virus. "Dengue is not simply an uncomfortable illness—it can be debilitating and, in severe cases, life-threatening," said Peter Jiang, PhD, director of Hillsborough County Mosquito Management and the president of the Florida Mosquito Control Association, (FMCA). "It is sometimes called 'break-bone fever' because of the intense muscle, joint and bone pain it can cause. For Floridians, it is an important reminder that mosquito control is not just about preventing a nuisance; it is an essential public health service that helps protect our residents and communities from mosquito-borne disease." The number of human cases is expected to increase in the state's weekly report. Florida's summertime climate is perfect for mosquitoes, and an influx of visitors – especially in popular destinations like Tampa - enjoying a summer vacation raises the opportunities for diseases to spread. Florida is home to some of the nation's top mosquito control scientists and professionals. Coordinating with the Florida Department of Health surveillance efforts are focused on limiting the spread. "And while success is never final, every resident and visitor to our state should know that Florida remains safe and accessible," he said. "Scientists and highly trained mosquito control professionals work closely with health officials 24/7 to limit the spread of any diseases." The state Department of Health says that dengue can "present as a flu-like illness with severe muscle aches and joint pain, fever, and sometimes a rash." Symptoms appear within 15 days of being bitten by an infected Aedes aegypti mosquito. The disease is not contagious among humans. For more than 100 years, the FMCA has helped scientists and entomologists battle mosquitoes and educate the public about the multitude of dangerous diseases transmitted via a mosquito bite. Currently, Hillsborough County Mosquito Management continues to work closely with the Florida Department of Health and various county and city departments to provide boots on the ground mosquito safety education in areas of concern. Florida's mosquito control efforts focus on scientific discovery and inquiry, front-line deployment of personnel and technology, and a strong network of professionals sharing best practices and experiences. In addition to treating areas where mosquitoes are heavily concentrated, counties use sentinel animals to test for some mosquito-spread diseases. Aircraft and drones can be deployed to treat inaccessible areas. Every Floridian can help, especially during hot weather which speeds up the lifecycle of a mosquito. The American Mosquito Control Association has a summary of measures , calling them the three Ds of protection. Drain –Following a rain or storm, each household and property becomes a virtual resort for mosquitoes looking for a place to breed. Emptying any water in toys or planters or anywhere is a critical and easy step to slow or eliminate the opportunity for mosquitoes to lay eggs. Mosquitoes only need a teaspoon of water to breed, emptying even small accumulations of water outside can make a difference. Dress – Wearing light-colored, loose-fitting clothes are a deterrent to mosquitoes, long sleeves or pants provide even more protection. Defend – Use insect repellants that are registered by the U.S. Environmental Protection Agency, especially those with DEET. They pose little to no risk for humans but are powerful shields against mosquitoes. There are 67 mosquito control programs in the state, including 15 independent districts that partner with each other and local departments of health to share important news, resources and scientific studies. They also stand ready to respond to any Floridian's request for local mosquito treatment. For more information about the FMCA please click here. And for teachers and parents, the FMCA has resources to inform children about the science and background of mosquitoes and efforts to control them here. CONTACT: Alia Faraj-Johnson SOURCE Florida Mosquito Control Association 21% more press release views with Request a Demo × Modal title

B routine priorityPublic-health news

'Break-bone' dengue virus numbers climbing, threat closing in on SW Florida's

News Brief
Dengue fever, known as 'break-bone' virus, is spreading in Florida, with most cases concentrated in the Tampa and Miami areas. While no locally acquired cases have been reported in Southwest Florida, officials are preparing for potential outbreaks. The virus is transmitted solely by the Aedes aegypti mosquito, which thrives in standing water around homes. Over 40 confirmed cases of locally transmitted dengue have been documented in Florida, with more than 30 in the Tampa area. The Florida Health Department and mosquito control districts are urging residents to take precautions, especially as the near-term forecast predicts rain in the Naples area, which could lead to increased mosquito activity. The Collier Mosquito Control District is actively monitoring the situation and ready to respond if dengue virus is detected in the region.
Original source text (English)
Schedule Football Basketball Recruiting Big Ten Sports BuckeyesXtra Newsletter HEALTH 'Break-bone' dengue virus numbers climbing, threat closing in on SW Florida's Chad Gillis Fort Myers News-Press & Naples Daily News Updated Aug. 31, 2026, 10:04 a.m. ET Dengue fever, also known as "break-bone" virus, is spreading in Florida, with most cases concentrated in the Tampa and Miami areas. The virus is spread solely by the Aedes aegypti mosquito, which thrives in standing water around human homes. While no locally acquired cases have been reported in Southwest Florida, officials urge residents to take precautions. In some places it's known as the "break-bone" virus due to the extreme pain it can cause. Dengue is on a lot of minds in Florida, and Southwest Florida is smack in the middle of the state's two highest concentration of infected residents ― Tampa and Miami. "Dengue fever is called break-bone fever because of how painful it can be," said Keira Lucas deputy executive director of the Collier Mosquito Control District. "And if you get it a second time, it can be deadly." Although no locally contracted cases of dengue fever have been reported in Southwest Florida, mosquito control districts and the Florida Health Department have been preparing for more instances of the potentially deadly virus. "There are more than 40 confirmed cases of locally acquired dengue fever in Florida, but none of them have been in the Collier area,' said Keira Lucas, "But more than 30 of those have been in the Tampa area." The Tampa area is less than a three-hour car drive from the Fort Myers-Naples area, and Lucas said people who travel to the Tampa area should pay special attention to mosquito activity until conditions improve. "We're starting to see more and more locally acquired cases popping up and several counties are under mosquito alerts for dengue," she said. "We want to be proactive. And if our residents are traveling to the Tamps area, take precautions when it comes to mosquito bites." Dengue fever is found in warm, tropical regions, and its spread solely by the Aedes aegypti mosquito. It was present in Florida in the 1930s but was not a health concern here for about 75 years. "Dengue was endemic to the state of Florida but it was eradicated," Lucas said. "And the mosquito that can spread dengue lives in close association with humans. It lives around people's homes and it likes to bite people." The virus returned to the Sunshine State in 2009 and 2010, when 85 cases (some being locally transmitted) were documented in Key West. Since then, dengue fever has been a concern, particularly in south Florida. "In 2011 and 2012, only isolated cases of local (dengue) transmission were reported in Florida, none were reported in Key West," says a report from National Library of Medicine. "In 2013, a new outbreak occurred, but this time in Martin County about 275 miles north of Key West with 22 reported cases." The mosquito that just loves us, sometimes to death Aedes aegypti is a species that has evolved alongside human beings for thousands of years. They really need two things: human blood and standing water. "Its habitat is artificial containers," Lucas said. "Bird baths, buckets, even something as small as a bottle cap can breed hundreds or thousands of Aedes aegypti mosquitoes." Lucas said any standing water around residential areas is fair game for this species as it has adapted to our behaviors. Humans build and keep containers, and sometimes we leave them outside during the rainy season. "It's a good time to go around the home and dump containers of standing water and can use larvicide," she said. "Dengue is only spread by mosquitoes, so it bites another human and then another human. There are no birds involved, like with West Nile virus." West Nile is one of several mosquito-borne viruses, and the others include: eastern equine encephalitis, St. Louis encephalitis, Zika, chikungunya, and yellow fever. Viscous saltwater mosquitoes still problematic in Collier One of the first signs that summer is coming in Southwest Florida is when hordes of saltmarsh mosquitoes take to the air and fly up to 60 miles in search of a blood meal. They typically become problematic during high tide phases in May and tend to become less active as the summer continues. Saltmarsh mosquito eggs are found in sandy, muck-like conditions found along most of the West coast of Florida, from the Tampa area to Naples to Flamingo in Everglades National Park. The saltwater mosquitoes have millions of acres of habitat, and treating them is difficult and sometimes impossible. "We are still seeing an extended saltmarsh season," Lucas said. "The drought conditions can really favor the saltmarsh mosquitoes because we're relying on rain to stick around and allow predators into those areas." SW Florida is home to more than 50 mosquito species While the freshwater Aedes aegypti doesn't travel further than a few hundred feet in its entire life, the saltmarsh variety can travel long distances to find blood meals, a requirement for laying the next horde. "Saltmarsh can travel 60 miles with wind," Lucas said. "We're constantly battling mosquitoes that come from our swamps and the extensive preserve lands we have." Lucas said the freshwater mosquitoes that live near and on our homes may be triggered soon as the near-term forecast calls for rain in the Naples area. "We're expecting rain in the next couple of days and that means outside containers are going go fill up," Lucas said. "And we may see dengue virus here in Collier County. And the district is ready to respond. We're already being proactive about keeping the population low" Dengue risk is high across the globe Dengue fever is common in the Caribbean, Central America, South America, the Middle East, Africa, Southeast Asia, and Pacific Islands, according to the Center for Disease Control and Prevention, or CDC. The CDC says just under half of people on the planet (about 4 billion people) live in areas where dengue is a risk. Chad Gillis is an environment reporter and can be reached by email at cgillis@news-press.com Please support local community journalism and stay informed about Southwest Florida news by subscribing to The News-Press and Naples Daily News; download the free News-Press or Naples Daily News app, and sign up for daily briefing email newsletter, food & dining and growth & development newsletters here and here. Dengue Burden Among Children Across Eight Endemic Asian and Latin American Countries - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
The news report from Bioengineer.org, dated August 28, 2026, discusses a study on the dengue burden among children in eight endemic Asian and Latin American countries. The report highlights the focus on understanding the disease's impact on children but does not provide specific case counts, deaths, or risk levels. The study was presented as a scientific summary, and no details about investigation status, response measures, or confirmed outcomes were included. The report is part of a broader collection of science-related news, emphasizing the need for further research and analysis on dengue's effects in vulnerable populations.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

卫生官员称,奥兰治县确诊本地感染登革热病例 - FOX 35 Orlando

Locally acquired case of dengue confirmed in Orange County, health officials say - FOX 35 Orlando
新闻简报据卫生官员称,佛罗里达州奥兰治县已确诊一例本地感染的 登革热 病例。这是该县首例确诊病例,全州已有 40 多例确诊病例。这一确认是正在进行的公共卫生监测的一部分,官员们已经提供了有关登革热的体征、症状和预防的信息。目前尚未报道有关调查或具体应对措施的更多细节。该病例凸显了该地区对该地区蚊媒疾病保持警惕的必要性,特别是在蚊子季节仍在继续的情况下。登革热 是一种由蚊子传播的病毒性疾病,虽然该病例的具体细节仍在调查中,但这一确认强调了预防措施和公众意识的重要性。

事件

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Locally acquired case of dengue confirmed in Orange County, health officials say - FOX 35 Orlando

News Brief
A locally-acquired case of dengue fever has been confirmed in Orange County, Florida, according to health officials. This marks the first confirmed case in the county, with more than 40 cases reported statewide. The confirmation comes as part of ongoing public health monitoring, and officials have provided information about the signs, symptoms, and prevention of dengue. No further details on the investigation or specific response measures have been reported. The case highlights the need for vigilance against mosquito-borne diseases in the region, especially as the mosquito season continues. Dengue fever is a viral illness transmitted by mosquitoes, and while the exact details of the case remain under investigation, the confirmation underscores the importance of preventive measures and public awareness.
